I don't know if it's going to last longer than that either. I'm just trying to get through my shifts at work right now without losing it. I haven't really thought that far ahead.\n\nDoctor: When you think about these symptoms you're experiencing now—the flashbacks, the emotional numbness, the trouble at work—do you think it's more likely they'll be mostly gone within the next month, or do you think they'll still be significantly affecting you after a month has passed?\n\nPatient: Honestly, like I was just saying... I really just don't know. I feel like we keep coming back to this, but I can't give you a timeline. I hope it's all gone in a month, but I have no idea if it actually will be. I'm really just trying to make it through my shifts right now without falling apart. I just don't know what to expect.\n\nDoctor: I know you're uncertain about the exact timeline, but let me ask it this way: do you think it's more likely than not that you'll still be having these flashbacks and emotional numbness a month from now, or do you think they're more likely to be mostly gone by then?\n\nPatient: I really... I just don't know how to answer that. I get that you're trying to figure out how long this will last, but I honestly have no clue. I'm not trying to be difficult, I just... I don't know if it's more likely or less likely. I'm just trying to get through today.\n\nDoctor: If you're still having these flashbacks and feeling emotionally numb a month from now, would that surprise you, or does that feel like something that could realistically happen?\n\nPatient: I guess... no, it wouldn't surprise me. I don't want that to be the case, obviously, but it wouldn't shock me if it is.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Was it sudden, or did it develop gradually over time?\n\nPatient: It was pretty sudden. Um, it's been going on for about three weeks now. It started right after... well, ever since a pallet collapsed at work and injured my coworker. I've just felt this way since that happened.\n\nDoctor: Since the pallet collapse, have you been having unwanted thoughts or images about that incident that keep coming back to you, even when you don't want them to?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. It's... out of nowhere, I'll just see that pallet coming down again, or I'll hear the crash in my head. It's especially bad at work. Like, if a forklift beeps or somebody drops something in the warehouse, it just flashes right back, and my stomach just turns.\n\nDoctor: Since the pallet collapse, have you been trying to avoid going to work, or staying away from certain areas of the warehouse, or avoiding things that remind you of what happened?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really done that. I mean, I still go to work every day, and I haven't tried to stay away from any particular aisles or anything. I just do my job. It's not like I'm trying to avoid the warehouse or the pallets. stack leaning and people yelling, but there’s this blank spot right after it fell where I know I must have moved or called for help, and I can’t piece it together no matter how hard I try.\n- key: I don't know which is more likely. I mean, obviously I want them to be gone, but sitting here right now, I just don't know what's going to happen. I just can't predict it for you."
